1-10 月财政数据点评

10 月当月政府性基金支出同比增速加快   
10 月公共预算收入同比增速加快。个人所得税对 10 月当月税收贡献明显。
公共财政仍有待向“稳增长”倾斜。10 月新增财政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9
月明显抬升，有望对 11-12 月政府性基金支出形成支撑。 
 10 月公共财政收入 23379.0 亿元，同比增长 3.4%，增速较 9 月转正加快
3.5 个百分点；其中当月税收收入 21916.0 亿元，同比增长 5.9%，增速较
9 月明显加快 5.7 个百分点；非税收入 1463.0 亿元，同比下滑 23.6%，降
幅较 9 月走扩 22.5 个百分点。 
 10 月全国一般公共预算支出 17843.0 亿元，同比增长 0.5%，增速较 9 月
加快 0.4 个百分点；其中，地方公共财政支出 14055.0 亿元，同比增长 0.1%，
增速较 9 月转正加速 0.9 个百分点。 
“稳增长”方面，当月公共财政支出投向基建领域的占比为 18.2%，较 9
月回落 1.7 个百分点；具体来看，10 月公共财政投向农林水事务的支出
同比增长 6.4%，增速较上月加速 6.6 个百分点，是唯一正增长的基建相
关支出分项；当期公共财政投向城乡社区事务及交通运输领域的支出同
比增速均为负，公共财政仍有待向基建领域倾斜。 
 10 月中央政府性基金收入 875.0 亿元，同比增长 155.8 %，增速较 9 月明
显改善 142.1 个百分点；地方政府性基金收入 3238.0 亿元，同比下滑
5.2%，降幅较 9 月收窄 13.3 个百分点；国有土地使用权出让收入 2439.0
亿元，同比下降 9.0%，降幅收窄 14.2 个百分点。 
 10 月政府性基金支出 6992.0 亿元，同比增长 17.2%，增幅较 9 月明显改
善 29.5 个百分点；中央政府性基金支出 537.0 亿元，同比增长 13.3%，增
速较 9 月加快 5.8 个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 6455.0 亿元，同
比增长 17.5 %，增速较 9 月明显加快 30.6 个百分点；国有土地使用权出
让收入相关支出 2354.0 亿元，同比下降 0.6%，降幅较 9 月收窄 18.1 个百
分点。 
 10 月政府性基金支出同比增速加快。1-10 月广义财政支出累计同比降幅
为 2.9%，降幅较 9 月收窄 0.6 个百分点，但仍相对偏弱。中央财政支出
在 1-10 月广义财政支出中占比 13.7%，仍是重要支撑。1-10 月广义中央
财政支出同比下滑 5.7%，降幅较 9 月有所收窄，但对当期广义财政支出
同比增速仍有拖累。 
从不同预算看，首先，1-10 月一般公共预算支出在全年公共预算支出中
占比 76.0%，支出力度整体不弱，持平于去年同期；但 10 月当月支出在
全年公共预算支出中占比为 5.9%，仍低于 2024-2025 年同期水平，10 月
公共财政支出节奏仍较克制。其次，10 月政府性基金支出同比增长
17.2%，增速明显加快。 
融资方面，10 月政府债务融资节奏加快，当月社融口径政府债融资规模
1.1 万亿元，较去年同期增加 6983.0 亿元；值得一提的是，10 月新增财
政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9 月明显抬升，有望对 11-12 月政府性基金支
出形成支撑。 
 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

资料来源：wind，中银证券

10 月公共预算收入同比增速加快 
财政部发布数据，2026 年 1-10 月，全国一般公共预算收入 195671.0 亿元，同比增长 4.9%，增速（较
前三季度，下同）放缓 0.2 个百分点。其中，中央一般公共预算收入 88036.0 亿元，同比增长 7.5%，
增速放缓 0.8 个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入 107635.0 亿元，同比增长 2.9%，增速小幅加快
0.2 个百分点。 
单月数据方面，10 月公共财政收入 23379.0 亿元，同比增长 3.4%，增速较 9 月转正加快 3.5 个百分
点；其中当月税收收入 21916.0 亿元，同比增长 5.9%，增速较 9 月明显加快 5.7 个百分点；非税收
入 1463.0 亿元，同比下滑 23.6%，降幅较 9 月走扩 22.5 个百分点。

图表 2.财政收入累计同比增速表现  图表 3. 税收、非税收入当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

个人所得税对 10 月当月税收贡献明显。首先，国内增值税仍是占比第一大的税收品种。10 月当月，
国内增值税同比下滑 0.1%，增速较 9 月有所下滑，我们认为，增值税同比增速短期或受企业采购、
税款入库时点错位等因素影响，单月波动或属正常现象。值得注意的是，10 月 PPI、工业增加值当
月同比分别增长 5.7%和 4.8%，有望支撑当月工业企业产值，后续国内增值税同比增速有望回升。 
其次，10 月进口环节增值税和消费税同比增长 17.8%，拉动当月税收同比增速 2.4 个百分点；据海
关总署数据显示，10 月我国进口同比增速为 41.6%，对进口环节增值税收入形成支撑。 
此外，10 月国内消费税收入同比增长 4.1%，拉动当月税收同比增速 0.4 百分点；值得说明的是，消费税
收入尚不具备持续修复的基础， 10 月社零同比增速仅为 0.4%，较 9 月放缓，国内需求仍较弱势。 
其他权重税种方面，10 月个人所得税收入同比增长 89.5%，对当月税收同比增速的正贡献为 10.4 个
百分点，贡献明显，10 月个人所得税增量主要来自离岸信托个人所得税补缴。

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 3

图表 4. 权重税种收入累计同比增速表现  图表 5.地产相关税种累计同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

公共财政仍有待向“稳增长”倾斜 
2026 年 1-10 月，全国一般公共预算支出 228042.0 亿元，同比增长 1.0%，增幅（较前三季度，下同）
持平；其中，中央一般公共预算本级支出 36608.0 亿元，同比增长 5.4%，增幅收窄 0.4 个百分点；
地方一般公共预算支出 191434.0 亿元，同比增长 0.2%，增幅持平。

图表 6.财政支出累计同比增速表现  图表 7. 2026 年 1-10 月财政支出同比增速结构变化

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

单月数据方面，10 月全国一般公共预算支出 17843.0 亿元，同比增长 0.5%，增速较 9 月加快 0.4
个百分点；其中，地方公共财政支出 14055.0 亿元，同比增长 0.1%，增速较 9 月转正加速 0.9 个
百分点。 
“稳增长”方面，当月公共财政支出投向基建领域的占比为 18.2%，较 9 月回落 1.7 个百分点；具体
来看，10 月公共财政投向农林水事务的支出同比增长 6.4%，增速较上月加速 6.6 个百分点，是唯一
正增长的基建相关支出分项；当期公共财政投向城乡社区事务及交通运输领域的支出同比增速均为
负，公共财政仍有待向基建领域倾斜。 
此外，教育、社保就业支出维持较强刚性，均仍为 10 月公共财政支出的重点方向，占比分别为 14.8%
和 15.8%。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 4

图表 8. 社保就业相关支出累计同比增速表现  图表 9.基建领域相关支出累计同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

土地收入对 1-10 月地方政府性基金收入仍有拖累 
2026 年 1-10 月，全国政府性基金预算收入 29123.0 亿元，同比下降 15.5%，降幅（较前三季度，下
同）收窄 3.1 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金预算收入 4557.0 亿元，同比增长 25.9%，
增幅加速 13.5 个百分点；地方政府性基金预算本级收入 24566.0 亿元，同比下滑 20.4%，降幅收窄
1.9 个百分点，其中，国有土地使用权出让收入 18526.0 亿元，同比下降 25.8%，降幅收窄 2.1 个百
分点，但需注意的是，土地收入对 1-10 月地方政府性基金收入仍有较明显拖累，负贡献为 20.9 个百
分点。 
单月数据方面，10 月中央政府性基金收入 875.0 亿元，同比增长 155.8 %，增速较 9 月明显改善 142.1
个百分点；地方政府性基金收入 3238.0 亿元，同比下滑 5.2%，降幅较 9 月收窄 13.3 个百分点；国
有土地使用权出让收入 2439.0 亿元，同比下降 9.0%，降幅收窄 14.2 个百分点。

图表 10. 政府性基金预算收入当月规模表现  图表 11. 地方政府性基金预算收入当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

支出方面，2026 年 1-10 月，全国政府性基金预算支出 69746.0 亿元，同比下滑 13.8%，降幅（相较
于前三季度，下同）收窄 2.4 个百分点；中央本级政府性基金支出 4229.0 亿元，同比下降 50.6%，
降幅较上月收窄 3.8 个百分点，中央财政支出力度相对温和；地方政府性基金预算支出 65517.0 亿元，
同比下降 9.4%，降幅收窄 2.2 个百分点，国有土地使用权出让收入相关支出 27919.0 亿元，同比下
降 17.3%，降幅收窄 1.2 个百分点。

### Page 5

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 5

单月数据方面，10 月政府性基金支出 6992.0 亿元，同比增长 17.2%，增幅较 9 月明显改善 29.5 个百
分点；中央政府性基金支出 537.0 亿元，同比增长 13.3%，增速较 9 月加快 5.8 个百分点；地方政府
性基金预算支出 6455.0 亿元，同比增长 17.5 %，增速较 9 月明显加快 30.6 个百分点；国有土地使用
权出让收入相关支出 2354.0 亿元，同比下降 0.6%，降幅较 9 月收窄 18.1 个百分点。

图表 12. 政府性基金预算支出当月规模表现  图表 13. 政府性基金预算支出当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

10 月政府性基金支出同比增速加快。1-10 月广义财政支出累计同比降幅为 2.9%，降幅较 9 月收窄
0.6 个百分点，但仍相对偏弱。中央财政支出在 1-10 月广义财政支出中占比 13.7%，仍是重要支撑。
1-10 月广义中央财政支出同比下滑 5.7%，降幅较 9 月有所收窄，但对当期广义财政支出同比增速仍
有拖累。 
从不同预算看，首先，1-10 月一般公共预算支出在全年公共预算支出中占比 76.0%，支出力度整体
不弱，持平于去年同期；但 10 月当月支出在全年公共预算支出中占比为 5.9%，仍低于 2024-2025
年同期水平，10 月公共财政支出节奏仍较克制。其次，10 月政府性基金支出同比增长 17.2%，增速
明显加快。 
融资方面，10 月政府债务融资节奏加快，当月社融口径政府债融资规模 1.1 万亿元，较去年同期增
加 6983.0 亿元；值得一提的是，10 月新增财政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9 月明显抬升，有望对 11-12
月政府性基金支出形成支撑。 
风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不及预期。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少，道瓊工業指數上漲 0.02%，收 52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收 7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓，資金流向非科技股及公債：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。
二、 下跌原因分析：

1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明 AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨 AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視，以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力。後續 10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲，顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%，第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比，反映目前市場主要擔憂 AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減 AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業，尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興 AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與 Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

不另行通知。
6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察，中期仍看好具基本面支撐的 AI 產業：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡。投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性，中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

 評論及分析 (Overall Summary) 
NVDA FY3Q27 營收、獲利及 Q4 財測都優於市場預期。財報公布後，股價
盤後一度上漲約 4%；董事會主席在法說會上表明不讓黃仁勳回任，漲幅一
度收斂。隨後暫代執行長 Kress 將 FY2028 營收成長指引由~70% 上修至
~75%，盤後漲幅再度擴大近 5%。一掃市場原先對公司可能會撤回長期展
望的擔憂；加上 Vera Rubin 創下公司史上最快放量，世代轉換十分順利。 
展望後市，市場普遍認為，AI 算力需求仍受供給限制，看好公司 FY2028 營
運能見度。CPU、網通與每座資料中心的產品含量提升，有助抵銷 ASIC 市
占提升的壓力；以 CY2027 本益比衡量，NVDA 評價仍低於費半。惟後續變
數在於 CEO 接班、美國新出口管制，以及相關調查與訴訟風險。整體而
言，基本面動能延續，惟治理與政策風險是評價折價的主因。

元；自由現金流 437.8 億美元，較上季的 213.4 億美元明顯回升。本季以庫
藏股與股利合計返還股東 281 億美元（庫藏股約 220 億美元、現金股利約 
60 億美元），創歷史新高。董事會於 9 月核准增加 1,500 億美元庫藏股額
度，季末剩餘額度約 2,273 億美元，計畫於 FY2028 年底前執行完畢。

Document 100: 2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/3 
2027 年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート 
～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確認～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 経団連による、2027 年春闘に向けての経労委報告原案が判明した。高い賃上げの「社会全体
への浸透」を掲げており、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中
小企業などにも広げることに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。また、筒井経団連会長の発言か
らは、ベア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針が維持されていることも確認できる。 
○ 流通やサービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンも定昇込み６％、ベア４％
など、前年並みの高い賃上げ要求を掲げた。労使とも、賃上げに向けての姿勢にトーンダウ
ンはみられない。 
○ 一方、賃上げ率のさらなる加速を示唆する材料は現時点で多くない。連合の要求目安は前年
より強まったものの、経団連は賃上げ率の加速までは示していないとみられ、ＵＡゼンセン
の要求率は前年と同水準である。2027 年春闘では、５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持でき
るかが焦点となる。 
○ 日銀の植田総裁は、2027 年春闘で２％のインフレ率を前提とした賃金設定が行われることの
重要性を指摘した。2026 年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化するなかでも高い賃上げが続くかど
うかは、２％の物価安定目標の定着や今後の金融政策を判断するうえで重要となる。

経労委報告原案は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」を掲げる 
10 月22 日のレポート（2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも
昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所）では、連
合の基本構想や経団連の経労委報告骨子案などをもとに、2027 年春闘に向けた賃上げ姿勢は労使
双方とも前年からのトーンダウンはみられないと評価したが、その後、経団連の経労委報告原案
やＵＡゼンセンの要求方針素案が明らかになった。本稿では、これらの新たな材料を踏まえ、春
闘の初動のモメンタムをアップデートする。 
10 月30 日、経団連が 2027 年春闘に向けて取りまとめる「経営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労
委報告）」の原案内容が報道された。報道によれば、原案では賃上げの力強いモメンタムを継続

### Page 2

2/3 
し、その動きを社会全体に浸透させるために取り組みを強化する方針が示された。特に、中小企
業が継続的・計画的な賃上げを実施できるかが重要とされ、適正な価格転嫁に加え、大企業が持
つ人材や技術を活用して中小企業の収益力向上を支援する必要性が指摘された。また、ＡＩの普
及を踏まえ、働き手が生み出した成果をどのように評価し、賃金などの処遇へ反映するかについ
て、見直しを求めている。生産性向上によって生み出された付加価値を賃金にどう配分するか
は、今後の労使交渉においても重要な課題となるだろう。 
経団連はこれまで、2023 年を賃上げの「起点」、2024 年を「加速」、2025 年を「定着」、2026 年
を「さらなる定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の原案では「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられて
おり、大企業を中心に高い賃上げが定着しつつあるなか、その動きを中小企業などにも広げるこ
とに重点を置く姿勢がうかがえる。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告でも、中小企業における構造的
な賃上げや適正な価格転嫁の重要性が強調されていたが、今回の原案では、大企業による人材・
技術面での支援にも言及するなど、賃上げの裾野を広げるための取り組みをより具体的に示して
いる。 
賃上げに対する経営側の基本姿勢も維持されている。2026 年春闘向けの経労委報告では、ベア
実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードと位置付けていたが、筒井経団連会長は今年９月７日の記
者会見でも、「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付けていきたい」と述べて
いる。ベアに対する従来の方針が維持されていることは重要だ。今春以降のイラン情勢悪化を受
け、原材料費や物流費の上昇によって企業の賃上げ余力が圧迫されることが懸念されていたが、
少なくとも現時点で経団連が賃上げに慎重な姿勢へ転じた様子はうかがえない。

UAゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求を維持 
労働側でも、主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針が明らかになり始めた。10 月 27 日、流通やサ
ービス、繊維などの労働組合で構成されるＵＡゼンセンは、2027 年春闘の要求方針素案を公表し
た。正社員組合員については、定昇込みで６％、ベア等の賃上げ分で４％を基本とし、賃金水準
が最低基準に満たない組合などでは格差是正分を加えて７％の賃上げを要求する。また、短時間
組合員については、制度昇給分に加えて５％、制度昇給分が明確でない場合には７％を目安とす
るとのことである。 
これらの要求水準は、いずれも 2026 年春闘から変わっていない。６～７％という要求水準は依
然として高いが、要求率を前年からさらに引き上げたわけではないことには注意が必要だ。連合
は 2027 年春闘に向けた基本構想で「６％中心、５％以上」と前年より強い要求目安を示したが、
実際には前年の段階で、すでに６％程度の賃上げを要求していた産別組織もある。今回の連合の
方針には、こうした既存の要求水準を追認する側面もあり、ＵＡゼンセンの要求方針はその一例
といえる。

前年からのトーンダウンはみられていないが、加速しているわけでもない  
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは引き続き良好と評価できる。経団連はベ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ア実施の検討をスタンダードとする従来の方針を維持し、高い賃上げを中小企業などにも広げる
ことで「社会全体への浸透」を目指す姿勢を示している。労働側でも、連合が前年より強い要求
目安を掲げており、ＵＡゼンセンも前年並みの高い要求水準を維持した。イラン情勢悪化に伴う
コスト増加が懸念されるなかでも、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢に前年からのトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
もっとも、賃上げ姿勢が慎重化していないことと、賃上げ率がさらに高まることは別問題であ
る。経団連が重視しているのは高い賃上げの継続とその裾野の拡大であり、賃上げ率の加速を示
唆しているわけではないだろう。また、ＵＡゼンセンの賃上げ要求は高いが、要求率自体は前年
から据え置かれている。連合の要求目安は強まったものの、現時点で賃上げ率の加速を見込むだ
けの材料は揃っていない。エコノミストの予想を集計するＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査（10 月調
査）でも、2027 年春闘の賃上げ率予想は4.96％とされており、前年実績の 5.18％から小幅に鈍化
するとの見方となっている（数値は厚生労働省ベース）。 
当面の焦点は、５％近傍という高い賃上げ率を維持できるかどうかにある。もっとも、今後の
経済・物価情勢によっては企業の賃上げ余力や労働側の要求姿勢も変化し得るため、先行きを慎
重に見極める必要がある。当面、自動車総連や電機連合など主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針
が前年からどの程度変化するかが注目される。 
金融政策の観点からも2027 年春闘の動向は重要である。日銀の植田総裁は 10 月 30 日の記者会
見で、基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定の目標と概ね整合的な水準に達したとの認識を示し
た上で、2027 年春闘について、労使交渉でも２％のインフレ率が前提として織り込まれることが
必要との考えを示した。一方、2026 年の物価上昇率が過去２～３年より低くなる可能性を指摘
し、それが春闘の結果にどう影響するかを注視する姿勢も示している。 
ここ数年の春闘では、物価上昇に負けない賃上げを実現することが、高い賃上げ要求を支える
重要な要因となってきた。2026 年の物価上昇率は 2025 年と比べれば鈍化する見込みであり、こう
した観点からの賃上げ圧力はやや弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃金を設定するよう
になれば、足元の物価上昇率が低下しても安定的な賃上げは持続しやすくなる。日銀は基調的な
物価上昇率が２％に「到達」したと判断したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判
断していない。その意味でも、2027 年春闘は、２％の物価上昇を前提とした賃金設定がどの程度
定着しているかを確認する機会となる。春闘に向けた労使双方の姿勢や実際の賃上げ動向は、今
後の日銀の金融政策判断においても重要な材料となるだろう。

Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
European NDR: strong five-year
outlook in AI ASICs; OW
Alchip's strong growth potential is rooted in US CSPs' AI ASIC
replacement cycle. Even after the recent rally, our price target
still implies 46% upside.
Right after its  3Q26 earnings call, we took Alchip's CEO, Mr. Johnny Shen, and CFO,
Mr. Daniel Wang, to visit European investors for discussion of the company’s long-
term strategy and outlook. Here are key takeaways:

Entering another phase of fast growth in the coming 4-5 years, with good
profitability: Alchip is sticking with its back-end design service focus, which
dovetails with CSPs' strategy of COT (customer-owned tooling). The long-term
trend is for CSP and AI lab customers to own the architecture and front-end design,
while pursuing efficiency in back-end design and production service. That strategy
can maximize the tokens generated per dollar  of AI capex. Besides winning future

generations with a major customer (AWS, 2nm and 1.4nm), Alchip has secured a
second big customer and is very close to winning a project with another US CSP (we
think it should be Google –  TPU computing dies). We project US$10bn total revenue
in 2028 –  4x our US$2.4bn estimate for  2026. Management thinks US$20bn
revenue p.a. by the end of this decade is possible, if the balance sheet permits.
More discussion of the second customer: The company still cannot disclose the

name of the second customer given the NDA, but reiterated that it expects a US$1bn
revenue contribution in 2027. Alchip provides part of the design service for the end
customer, and the gross margin is reasonably good. Although we don’t rule out the
possibility of another design service provider in this ASIC project for IP or front-end
design, we maintain our view that the end customer is a US CSP that uses Alchip for
back-end design service. Our industry checks don't see a CoWoS booking for this

ASIC, so we think it could be related to AI networking.
Getting close to winning a third big customer: In our October 11 report, Solid 2027
and 2028 outlook with potential catalysts, we already indicated that Alchip is a
strong candidate for Google TPU computing die back-end design service.
Management continued to show high confidence in a new project win without
specifying customer name; it expects some NRE revenue to come by the end of
2027. We asked which customer (second or third) could make a bigger revenue

contribution 2-3 years down the road – management thinks the third customer may
show bigger revenue potential.
Alchip's value underpinned by major customer (AWS): This could be reflected in
higher allocation of Trainium3 in 2027 and better business terms for Trainium4
(profits to grow with the volume upside). Management also expects 1.4nm design
activities to kick off some time in 2027 –  the key to five-year growth visibility.
Please see the following section for investors' feedback and our views.

### Page 2

Idea
2
Stronger revenue   guidance for 2027 and 2028
European investors' feedback
1. Alchip was somewhat overlooked in the AI ASIC market over the past two years,
compared with MediaTek and GUC, which continued to win new projects. Winning
second and third big customers is considered a re-rating driver – it mitigates the
concentration in a single customer (AWS).
2.  Meanwhile, European investors generally agree that AI ASICs should gain share
against GPUs, given tighter AI capex budgets. We share the view expressed in

recent comments from Alchip and MediaTek management: certain AI ASICs that
companies help to design provide very competitive TCO (total cost of ownership).
3. It is hard to track ASIC supply chain data points in real time, but the ASIC story
and Alchip's industry position can justify a long-term investment. Changes in
short-term data points wouldn't stop European investors from investing in Alchip.
4.  On valuation, investors think Alchip's 2028e implied P/E of 11x based on our EPS

estimate is relatively attractive vs. other global ASIC design service peers.
Considering this feedback, we think Alchip stock should  continue to re-rate: The stock
has been volatile, rebounding 50% since November 6 after a 40% pullback over October
12 to November  6. (The TAIEX was  +10% and -20% over those periods.) Based on our
estimates, P/E is now 17x for 2027 and 11x for 2028, lower than its historical average of
28x one-year forward P/E since 2019.

Key catalysts could be:
• November 30 –  AWS re:Invent conference with Trainium4 announcement;
• Supply chain identification of the second big customer's name and order potential;
• Confirmation of Google computing die back-end design service;
• Bigger Trainium3 revenue ramp in 3Q27 vs. 2Q27.

and terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bear case value of NT$2,465 and bull case value of NT$6,880 are also unchanged.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q26 earnings beat on margins: Better GM of 32.5% (+1.8ppt QoQ) and well-
controlled opex of NT$3,349mn (opex ratio 12.0%) led to an operating profit of NT
$5,720mn (OPM 20.5%), 6%/11% higher than MSe/cons. at NT$5,390mn (OPM
18.9%)/NT$5,142mn (OPM 18.7%).
Power interconnects playing an increasingly important role: We estimate that
power was 65-70% of its HPC revenue in 3Q26 vs. ~50% in the past two years,
which should bring in tailwinds for its blended GM, as power interconnects in

general carry higher margins than data ones. Looking forward, we expect the
increasing power consumption in AI clusters to continue drive the growth from both
volume and spec upgrades in this space. Moreover, future power delivery
architecture could also add incremental opportunities for Bizlink.
Data interconnects diversify into optical: While the market has been concerned
about its data interconnect business recently owing to the focus on the copper

camp, we’d point out that the revenue mix from data interconnects decreased to 30-
35% of HPC in 3Q26 (or 15% of total revenue), per our estimates. Looking ahead, it
is diversifying into the optical space, including micro VCSEL based ALCs along with
its US partner, shuffle boxes/FAU alignment process for CPO and fiber patch cords.
Interplex contribution bigger than expected: Bizlink started to consolidate
Interplex from late Sep and it contributed US$67mn of revenue in Oct, or 18% of

Bizlink’s US$369mn (NT$11,846mn, +27% QoQ/+80% YoY). This is higher than the
market expectation of high single digit %. Our checks indicate that its margins are
above Bizlink’s corporate average, so the earnings might be over 20%, we estimate.
We believe this could make its offerings more comprehensive in the AI cluster and
Interplex could expand its business faster with the financial strength of Bizlink.
Stay OW: We think the growth outlook looks solid and is further strengthened by

the consolidation of Interplex. With the valuation of 11x 2027 P/E approaching the
trough valuation of 8x P/E post the recent pullback, we think risk reward looks
attractive. Stay OW with lower PT of NT$2,500 (18x 2027 P/E).

Results Summary
3Q26 earnings beat on margins
Off revenue of NT$27,875mn (+20% QoQ/+51% YoY), GM expanded by 1.8ppt to 32.5%,
vs. MSe/consensus at 32.0%/31.8%, on a more favorable revenue mix. Opex was also well
controlled at NT$3,349mn (opex ratio 12.0%) vs. MSe/consensus at NT$3,728mn (opex
ratio 13.1%)/NT$3,615mn (opex taio 13.1%), leading to an operating profit of NT$5,720mn
(OPM 20.5%), 6%/11% higher than MSe/consensus at NT$5,390mn (OPM 18.9%)/NT

$5,142mn (OPM 18.7%). With the non-op loss of NT$210mn, net profit came in at NT
$4,293mn (EPS NT$22.01), 7%/13% higher than MSe/consensus at NT$3,996mn (EPS NT
$20.67)/NT$3,789mn (EPS NT$19.27).

Idea
6
Valuation Methodology
Our new price target (base case scenario value) of  NT$2,500 (NT$3,665 previously) is
derived from 18x 2027e P/E (30x previously): We use price/earnings as our valuation
methodology because we believe it appropriately captures Bizlink's earnings growth
potential. We believe our target P/E multiple of 18x is justified by an earnings CAGR of
55%   over 2025-28, which implies a PEG of  0.3, lower than the average of 0.7 in 2019-24,

when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 13x for a 19% earnings CAGR. We use a
lower target multiple to factor in more conservative sentiment towards AI stocks.
Bull case scenario value – NT$4,100 (vs. prior 4,800): We apply a 30x P/E multiple (vs.
prior 40x) to our base case estimate for 2027. This scenario assumes stronger AEC/ALC
demand owing to broader adoption within AI servers along with spec upgrades.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,100 (vs. prior 1,760): We apply a 8x P/E multiple (vs.

prior 15x) to our base case estimate for 2027. This scenario assumes lukewarm data center
demand amid softening IT capex budgets and pricing pressure for low-barrier products in
other business segments.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Rachel L Zhang
Equity Analyst

Key Takeaways
Earlier this week, a key Japanese competitor issued a price increase notice to its
customers for electrodeposited copper foils.
This aligns with our  supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report.
We believe the  price hikes have positive implications for Co-Tech and expect Co-
Tech to follow with price increases as well.

However, since Co-Tech already implemented a ~10% price hike in October, we
don't expect it to announce a price hike of the same magnitude.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Document 260: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000 원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000 원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
No
vember 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

with demand, supply bottlenecks may
reinforce the investment case for the
companies providing the infrastructure
behind the AI buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

Document 312: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-23)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

Document 350: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP.  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP, and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP, although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

Key points
• The value of new dwelling finance
approvals excl. refinance dropped
15.5%qtr, the biggest quarterly decline
on records going back to 1991. Annual
growth swung to –18.1%yr. While the

decline was widely expected, it was
larger than the median forecast of -8.5%
and Westpac’s forecast of –10%.
• The quarterly drop was mostly driven
by lower volumes: total loan numbers
down 15%qtr (-21%yr). Smaller average
loan size contributed a further 0.5ppts
to the value decline in the quarter.
• Investors were the primary driver,
volumes in this segment down
over 30%qtr and the value of new
loans off by nearly a third.
• Borrowing by owner occupiers

significant but somewhat milder
declines, both the volume and value
of new loans down 5.3%qtr.
• More weakness looks likely for Q4.
